## [ENG-001] Supplier-payment void/reversal workflow missing
- Severity: High
- Category: Missing Feature
- Initiative: Financial Governance
- Complexity: L
- Affected Modules: SuppliersController, CommerceAccountingService, AccountingOutboxProcessor | CommerceAccountingService, SuppliersController | SuppliersController, SupplierPayment, AccountingOutbox
- Dependencies: ACC-003 (period lock), ACC-018 (adjustment reversal pattern)
- Source Documents: audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:99-105 (C-02/CV-02); audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-REMEDIATION-REPORT.md:134-140; accounts/FLOW-ACC-06-PAYMENTS.md:120-179 | audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:C-02; ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-REMEDIATION-REPORT.md:134-140 | `audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT-CHANGELOG.md:46-52` (C-02 PARTIALLY VERIFIED); `audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-REMEDIATION-REPORT.md:134-141`
- Description: `SuppliersController` can create supplier payments, but there is no `ReverseSupplierPayment`, `CancelSupplierPayment`, or supplier-payment reversal outbox path. If a supplier payment must be operationally unwound, there is no first-class symmetric accounting path; the only remedy is GL `ReverseJournalAsync`, unwired to any party-payment endpoint.
- Evidence: "Supplier payment creation exists... but no ReverseSupplierPayment, CancelSupplierPayment, or supplier-payment reversal outbox path was found." | "Supplier payment creation exists… but no `ReverseSupplierPayment`, `CancelSupplierPayment`, or supplier-payment reversal outbox path was found." | "C-02: 'no supplier-payment reversal command or outbox path found.'"
- Recommendation: Add an explicit supplier-payment void/reversal command posting a reversal/compensating journal through the existing posting/outbox infrastructure (do not reuse the order-refund architecture).
- Implementation Required: yes | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: no

## [ENG-002] Concurrent cashbox close race (no pessimistic/conditional update)
- Severity: Medium
- Category: Concurrency Concern
- Initiative: Financial Governance
- Complexity: M
- Affected Modules: CashboxController
- Dependencies: none
- Source Documents: audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:182-186 (M-02/F-10-01); audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-REMEDIATION-REPORT.md:142-148; audits/_scratch/AGENT-10-DAILYCLOSING.md:32-49 | audits/_scratch/AGENT-10-DAILYCLOSING.md:F-10-01; ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-REMEDIATION-REPORT.md:142-148
- Description: `CloseCashbox` reads/guards `IsActive` without a row lock or conditional `WHERE IsActive = true` update, so overlapping closures can both pass the guard, producing duplicate audit rows (no GL corruption, but operational/audit divergence).
- Evidence: "CloseCashbox starts a default transaction and reads/guards IsActive without row lock or conditional update." | "Two concurrent requests both read `IsActive=true`, both set `false`, both commit."
- Recommendation: Add provider-aware conditional update or row-version `WHERE IsActive = true` with a 0-affected-rows = already-closed conflict response.
- Implementation Required: yes | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: no

## [ENG-003] No configurable party-balance floor policy for adjustments
- Severity: Medium
- Category: Business Rule Gap
- Initiative: Financial Governance
- Complexity: M
- Affected Modules: LedgerService, PartyLedgerAdjustment
- Dependencies: none
- Source Documents: audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:201-205 (M-05/F-11-05); audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-REMEDIATION-REPORT.md:158-164; audits/_scratch/AGENT-11-ADJUSTMENTS.md:108-119
- Description: `LedgerService.ApplyPartyTransactionAsync` computes running balance with no floor; `PreventNegative` only guards GL accounts, not party columns. Adjustments can drive a party balance arbitrarily negative.
- Evidence: "LedgerService computes running balance without a floor... GL PreventNegative guards only GL accounts, not party columns."
- Recommendation: Define configurable floor rules per party/account type, then enforce in the party-mutation service (business-policy decision; implement only where required).
- Implementation Required: yes | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: no

## [ENG-004] Adjustments not linked to GL by stored journal id
- Severity: Medium
- Category: Reporting Gap
- Initiative: Financial Governance
- Complexity: M
- Affected Modules: PartyLedgerAdjustment, FinancialIntegrityService, AccountingOutboxProcessor
- Dependencies: none
- Source Documents: audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:207-211 (M-06/F-11-06); audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-REMEDIATION-REPORT.md:166-172; audits/_scratch/AGENT-11-ADJUSTMENTS.md:121-131
- Description: `PartyLedgerAdjustment` has no `JournalEntryId`; the GL link is reconstructed by `SourceType`/`SourceId`. Adds fragility to reconciliation and traceability.
- Evidence: "PartyLedgerAdjustment has no JournalEntryId; link validation reconstructs by SourceType/SourceId."
- Recommendation: Store posted `JournalEntryId` on successful adjustment posting, or expose a derived link status without migrating historical rows.
- Implementation Required: yes | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: no

## [ENG-006] No shared accounting-period / locked-period guard
- Severity: High
- Category: Governance Concern
- Initiative: Financial Governance
- Complexity: L
- Affected Modules: StatementOfAccountController, ExpensesController, FinancialPostingService, FinancialReportService, CashboxController | all financial write controllers/services | FinancialPostingService, StatementOfAccountController, ExpensesController, ReportsController

- Source Documents: audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:219-223 (M-08/F-11-08),249-253 (M-13/F-08-07),298-302 (M-21/CV-11); audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-REMEDIATION-REPORT.md:182-188,198-204 | audits/_scratch/AGENT-CROSS-VALIDATION.md:CV-11; ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:M-21 | `audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:369` (CV-11 VERIFIED MEDIUM); `accounts/FLOW-ACC-11-ACCOUNT-ADJUSTMENTS.md:146-156` (F-11-08)
- Description: No `IAccountingPeriodLock` or period service exists. Adjustments and expenses post without checking closed periods; reports read any date range. Period-lock ownership belongs to no flow.
- Evidence: "No period-lock service was found; adjustments and expenses do not check closed periods before writes." | "No shared `IAccountingPeriodLock` service is invoked by any financial write path." | "M-21/F-11-08: 'No shared accounting-period lock service was found and financial writes lack period checks.'"
- Recommendation: Add a shared accounting-period service invoked at all financial write paths (adjustments, expenses, supplier payments) and reject writes to closed periods.
- Implementation Required: yes | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: no

## [ENG-007] Party-balance source-of-truth split (AccountLedger vs cache vs SupplierTransactions mirror)
- Severity: Medium
- Category: Architecture Issue
- Initiative: Financial Governance
- Complexity: L
- Affected Modules: AccountLedger, Customer/Supplier, SupplierTransactions, StatementOfAccountController | StatementOfAccountController, LedgerService
- Dependencies: none
- Source Documents: audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:176-180 (M-01/F-09-03),286-290 (M-19/CV-08),309-312 (M-23/F-09-01); audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-REMEDIATION-REPORT.md:190-196 | audits/_scratch/AGENT-CROSS-VALIDATION.md:CV-08; ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:M-19
- Description: Party balance has three representations — `AccountLedger.RunningBalance` (authority), `Customer`/`Supplier.Balance` (cache), and `SupplierTransactions.BalanceAfter` (supplier auxiliary mirror). Supplier aux aggregates (`TotalPaid`/`TotalInvoiced`/`DueDate`) read from the mirror, unlike the customer path which uses `AccountLedgers`.
- Evidence: "Supplier balance is ledger-sourced, but TotalPaid/TotalInvoiced/DueDate use SupplierTransactions and PurchaseInvoices." | "Supplier outstanding sub-aggregates… read from `SupplierTransactions` mirror, not `AccountLedgers`" while customer branch uses `AccountLedgers`.
- Recommendation: Make `AccountLedger` the party display source and label/limit operational mirrors; finalize ownership cleanup as larger read-model work.
- Implementation Required: yes | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: no

## [ENG-008] Uneven async GL convergence visibility (no shared GL-converged signal)
- Severity: Medium
- Category: Monitoring Gap
- Initiative: Financial Governance
- Complexity: L
- Affected Modules: FinancialIntegrityService, Expense posting, AccountingOutboxProcessor, operator dashboards | FinancialIntegrityService, reporting UI
- Dependencies: ACC-001
- Source Documents: audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:168-174 (H-08/CV-06),200-204 (H-08/CV-10); audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-REMEDIATION-REPORT.md:198-204 | audits/_scratch/AGENT-CROSS-VALIDATION.md:CV-10; ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:M-11
- Description: POS sale posting is synchronous; expenses/adjustments post via async outbox. Order/refund posting statuses are updated, but expense posting lacks the same integrity scan/status exposure. Operators have no universal "outbox drained / GL converged" indicator across async financial sources.
- Evidence: "Convergence safeguards exist, but are inconsistent by flow." | "No endpoint surfaces whether the outbox has drained for a tenant/entity, and no per-tenant lag metric exists."
- Recommendation: Standardize a minimal convergence contract (posted/failed/pending status or shared outbox-lag/GL-converged endpoint) for every async financial source.
- Implementation Required: yes | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: no

- Severity: Medium
- Category: Missing Feature

- Description: `LedgerEntry` and `Account` carry only `decimal(18,4)` with no currency column; SOA/report DTOs carry no currency. Single-currency assumption pervades treasury, reports, and party ledgers.
- Evidence: "LedgerEntry has decimal amounts without currency; SOA/report DTOs carry no currency." | "F-08-08: 'LedgerEntry.DebitAmount/CreditAmount are decimal(18,4) with no currency.'"
- Recommendation: Treat as a target-market scope decision; implement only with a tenant currency model and FX policy. Do not implement unless multi-currency tenants are required.
- Implementation Required: yes | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: no

- Description: Remediation fixed daily-close reversed-source subtotals, but reversal disclosure is still inconsistent across reporting surfaces (financial reports net posted+reversed; daily close now uses posted only; expense reports read operational expenses before GL convergence). Expense reports can still diverge from GL/P&L during async lag.

## [ENG-013] Two-tier party/GL decoupling boundary under-documented
- Severity: Medium
- Category: Documentation Inconsistency
- Initiative: Financial Governance
- Complexity: S
- Affected Modules: SalesController, CommerceAccountingService, AccountLedger, GL
- Dependencies: none
- Source Documents: audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md:280-284 (M-18/CV-07); audits/_scratch/AGENT-06-PAYMENTS.md:193-222 (F-06-05)
- Description: For POS cash/card sales, the customer `AccountLedger` carries Sale/PaymentReceived entries (net zero) but the GL posts no AR(1410) line — only revenue and cash. AR control (1410) != POS customer `AccountLedger` totals for cash/card. The decoupling is documented but inconsistently emphasized, risking false future findings and reconciliation mistakes.
- Evidence: "For a cash POS sale the customer AccountLedger still carries a Sale and PaymentReceived entry, but the GL contains no AccountsReceivable line."
- Recommendation: Explicitly document which party-ledger entries are informational vs GL-control-account-affecting; add a note in FLOW-ACC-06 that cash/card POS `AccountLedger` is informational.
- Implementation Required: no | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: yes

- Category: Documentation Inconsistency
- Initiative: Financial Governance
- Complexity: S
- Affected Modules: OrderFulfillmentService, Payment Lifecycle
- Dependencies: none
- Source Documents: capabilities/pos-admin/order-management.md:749-752, 815-816; platform/order-runtime-final-architecture.md:551-553
- Description: Payment/fulfillment outbound progression is gated by `OrderOperationalFinancialAuthority`: non-COD positive-total orders cannot advance outbound until payment is a paid state AND (if required) the collection accounting journal is posted. Documentation that assumes fulfillment can proceed independently of payment is wrong.
- Evidence: "Documentation that assumes fulfillment can proceed independently of payment is wrong."
- Recommendation: Surface the financial gating rule in all fulfillment/order lifecycle docs.
- Implementation Required: no | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: yes

## [ENG-072] Four parallel ledgers with separate write paths (reconciliation risk)
- Severity: High
- Category: Technical Debt
- Initiative: Financial Governance
- Complexity: XL
- Affected Modules: Accounting, Reporting, CommerceHub finance, Party ledger
- Dependencies: ARC-016, ARC-017
- Source Documents: platform/reporting-runtime-final-architecture.md:977-983 (TD12); architecture/financial-architecture.md:118,169,207-212
- Description: `LedgerEntries` (GL), `AccountLedgers` (party), `SupplierTransactions`/`CustomerTransactions` (detail), and `CommerceHubFinancialTransactions` (marketplace) are maintained by independent writers. Party balances reconciled only via manual `RebuildPartyBalanceAsync`; supplier outstanding details read `SupplierTransactions`+`PurchaseInvoices` (a third model) that can drift from `AccountLedgers`.
- Evidence: "Four parallel ledgers, separate write paths … Party balances are reconciled only via a manual RebuildPartyBalanceAsync … can drift from AccountLedgers. Priority: High."
- Recommendation: Establish a single canonical GL + one derived party sub-ledger with enforced reconciliation jobs; reduce independent writers.
- Implementation Required: yes | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: no

- Description: `CloseCashbox` commits state then calls `AuditService.EnqueueLog` in a separate scope; on failure it falls back to an in-memory `ConcurrentQueue` that is lost on process exit. A closure can therefore commit with no durable audit record (F-10-03/M-04). Remediation keeps this DEFERRED.
- Evidence: "F-10-03: 'if both the immediate SQL write and the retry queue fail, the closure is recorded in state but leaves no audit trail.'"
- Recommendation: Persist closure audit inside the close transaction or via a durable audit outbox; alert on queue depth.

- Recommendation: Define one canonical cost authority (e.g. LOT/FIFO or weighted-average) and derive all three reports from it.

## [ENG-137] Dual carrier config runtime authority divergence (CarrierConfiguration vs CarrierAccount)
- Severity: High
- Category: Architecture Issue
- Initiative: CommerceHub & Storefront
- Complexity: L
- Affected Modules: CheckoutService, ShippingQuoteService, CommerceHubShippingService, ShipmentService, CarrierShipmentService, ShippingMethodService, ShippingAdministration
- Dependencies: none
- Source Documents: capabilities/commerce/shipping-management.md:290-295, 363-459, 541-560; platform/shipping-fulfillment-runtime-final-architecture.md:650, 660, 849-851, 860
- Description: Two independently modeled carrier config authorities exist. `CarrierConfiguration` (legacy) is read by checkout, quotes, CommerceHub shipping, shipment creation, carrier registration/cancellation/sync, and webhook trust. `CarrierAccount` (governance) is read only by storefront warehouse-scoped visibility filtering. A carrier enabled in one but blocked/inactive in the other yields inconsistent availability ("available in admin but rejected at checkout", "visible in selector but no quote"). No adapter enforces equivalence.
- Evidence: "A carrier can be enabled in CarrierConfiguration but inactive/blocked in CarrierAccount; checkout and quotes still see it as available … Merchants can observe inconsistent carrier availability between admin lifecycle screens, storefront method visibility, checkout acceptance, quote results, and shipment registration."
- Recommendation: Converge to ONE runtime authority — either promote `CarrierAccount` (and wire checkout/quote/registration/webhook to it) or explicitly demote it to governance/visibility-only and rename screens. Document both paths and block UI from presenting one as the other's source of truth until converged.
- Implementation Required: yes | Architecture Only: yes | Documentation Only: no

These are testing/automation-relevant but were never folded into any tracked backlog.

## [ENG-180] Tenant `IsActive` vs `Tenant.Status` dual source of truth (split-brain)
- Severity: Critical

- Initiative: Security & Identity
- Complexity: M
- Affected Modules: Tenant lifecycle, TenantResolutionMiddleware, TenantsController, SuperAdmin governance, Identity authority | Tenant resolution, `TenantSaveChangesInterceptor`, `PrivilegedRefreshTokenRevocationInterceptor`, `TenantsController`, SuperAdmin governance

- Source Documents: platform/superadmin-platform-final-architecture.md:694-704, 596-598; platform/identity-runtime-final-architecture.md:722-723; platform/merchant-platform-final-architecture.md (debt) | platform/identity-runtime-final-architecture.md §624, Technical Debt #2, Implementation Follow-up (Unify tenant status)
- Description: `Tenant.IsActive` (deprecated bool) coexists with `Tenant.Status` (enum). `TenantsController.SetTenantStatus` writes only `IsActive`; `TenantResolutionMiddleware` gates access on BOTH `IsActive && Status==Active`, so a tenant with `IsActive=true, Status=PendingSetup` still passes. `TenantRegistrationService` and `DevelopmentCommerceHubSeedService` dual-write both. SuperAdmin, Identity, and Merchant docs all flag this independently.
- Evidence: "Concrete split-brain: TenantsController.SetTenantStatus writes only tenant.IsActive … while TenantResolutionMiddleware gates access on both IsActive && Status==Active … a tenant with IsActive=true, Status=PendingSetup still passes" | "`SetTenantStatus` updates only the bool; interceptor and governance read different signals. Split-brain risk."
- Recommendation: Migrate all reads/writes to `Status` as sole signal; remove `IsActive` writes; update `TenantResolutionMiddleware` to gate on `Status` only.
- Implementation Required: yes | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: no

- Description: Webhook `AllowAnonymousTenant` controllers are the one sanctioned exception to tenant-middleware stripping. It is safe ONLY because the webhook trust pipeline establishes tenant scope from the signed payload + stored SQL records. A NEW anonymous-tenant endpoint without that pipeline is a critical scope defect (would resolve tenant from an untrusted source).
- Evidence: "Webhook `AllowAnonymousTenant` is the single sanctioned exception to tenant middleware stripping. It is safe ONLY because the webhook trust pipeline establishes tenant scope from the signed payload + stored SQL records. A new anonymous-tenant endpoint without that pipeline is a critical scope defect."
- Recommendation: Add a code/review gate that forces any `[AllowAnonymousTenant]` endpoint to demonstrate the webhook trust pipeline (signed payload + SQL record scope resolution) or it fails review.
- Implementation Required: yes | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: no

## [ENG-215] Service Marketplace global Elasticsearch index is tenant-UNSCOPED for discovery
- Severity: Informational
- Category: Known Limitation
- Initiative: Security & Identity
- Complexity: M

- Dependencies: SEC-017
- Source Documents: platform/service-marketplace-participation-runtime-final-architecture.md (Executive Summary, Core Discovery, Runtime Purpose)
- Description: The `marketplace_services` Elasticsearch index is intentionally tenant-UNSCOPED for read/search visibility (`Tenant filter REMOVED for Marketplace (Global visibility)`), so services from any tenant are globally discoverable. Engagement (request/chat/review/trust) is always re-scoped to current SQL tenant/profile authority before shown or mutated. The cross-tenant READ exposure is intentional but requires continuous monitoring that it never leaks into the write path.
- Evidence: "Global discovery is deliberate but read-only. The Elasticsearch service index (`marketplace_services`) is intentionally tenant-UNSCOPED for search visibility... But the engagement (request/chat/review/trust) is always re-scoped to current SQL tenant/profile authority before it is shown or mutated."
- Recommendation: Add an assertion/test that global discovery read never grants another tenant write authority over a provider's data; keep the re-scope enforcement verified.
- Implementation Required: no (monitoring + test coverage) | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: yes

- Recommendation: Route all dangerous primitives through the shared guards (`DerivativeProjectionMutationGuard`, `ProjectionEventFence`, `SqlTruthRebuildGuard`); add a lint/CI check that fails when a new raw primitive lacks an allowlist entry + assertion.

- Source Documents: capabilities/pos-admin/order-management.md:735-738, 813-814
- Description: Cancellation of an order with a pending PaymentIntent does NOT immediately cancel; it moves to OnHold, enqueues `PaymentCancellationOutboxMessage`, and defers inventory release until payment cancellation completes. The simpler "cancel releases inventory" phrasing hides this timing.
- Evidence: "A naive reader of 'cancel releases inventory' would be wrong about timing."
- Recommendation: Document the OnHold + deferred-release behavior explicitly wherever cancellation is described.
- Implementation Required: no | Architecture Only: no | Documentation Only: yes

### DCONF-001: CONFLICT-001: FLOW-ACC-06 scope vs order-payment runtime usage
- Conflicting Documents: accounts/FLOW-ACC-06-PAYMENTS.md:6 (scopes OUT order-payment runtime) vs audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md & task file list (used PaymentLifecycleService/gateway adapters as FLOW-ACC-06 evidence)
- Explanation: The canonical doc explicitly excludes the order-payment/CommerceHub runtime, yet the audit and task treated those files as the FLOW-ACC-06 subject, mixing ERP party payments with order payments.
- Possible Cause: Task file list generated from a query that did not honor the canonical doc's scope boundary.
- Recommended Resolution: Split ERP party payments and order/CommerceHub payment runtime into separate canonical flows/audit sections (see ACC-010).
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-002: CONFLICT-002: Party-balance source-of-truth split (three representations)
- Conflicting Documents: accounts/FLOW-ACC-01-CHART-OF-ACCOUNTS.md:35 (AccountLedger.RunningBalance = authority) vs accounts/FLOW-ACC-09-STATEMENT-OF-ACCOUNT.md (Customer.Balance cache) vs SupplierTransactions mirror (used for TotalPaid/TotalInvoiced)
- Explanation: Party balance is represented by the authoritative `AccountLedger`, a `Customer`/`Supplier` cached column, and a `SupplierTransactions` mirror; supplier aux aggregates read from the mirror while customer path reads `AccountLedger`.
- Possible Cause: Historical evolution of supplier aux aggregates before the unified party ledger.
- Recommended Resolution: Make `AccountLedger` the single display source; label/limit the cache and mirror as operational (see ACC-008).
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-003: CONFLICT-003: AccountSummary.IsTreasury mapping inconsistency (internal)
- Conflicting Documents: PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Accounting/FinancialReportService.cs:576 (IsTreasury = a.IsSystem) vs :589 (IsTreasury = account.IsTreasury)
- Explanation: One projection branch derives treasury flag from `IsSystem`; the other reads the account's own `IsTreasury`. Results can disagree.
- Possible Cause: Copy/paste projection where semantics differ.
- Recommended Resolution: Always source `IsTreasury` from `Account.IsTreasury` (see ACC-052).
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-004: CONFLICT-004: Affiliate accounting — doc says ledger-liability-only, code posts GL journals
- Conflicting Documents: audits/financial-completeness-audit.md:33 (affiliate commission = affiliate-ledger liability only, payout via orchestrator) vs implementation `AffiliateCommissionEarnedAccountingConsumer`/`AffiliateCommissionPaidAccountingConsumer` (post GL journals)
- Explanation: The architecture doc under-describes affiliate accounting; the implementation has MassTransit GL-posting consumers, creating both a doc contradiction and a dual-posting risk with the payout orchestrator.
- Possible Cause: Doc not updated after affiliate MassTransit consumers were added.
- Recommended Resolution: Reconcile doc with consumers; document production ownership to avoid double posting (see ACC-059).
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-005: CONFLICT-005: Reversed/not-yet-posted treatment differs across reporting surfaces
- Conflicting Documents: accounts/FLOW-ACC-08-FINANCIAL-REPORTS.md (financial reports net posted+reversed) vs accounts/FLOW-ACC-10-DAILY-CLOSING.md:369-427 (Z-Report counts Reversed source journals at face value in breakdowns) vs accounts/FLOW-ACC-07-EXPENSES.md (expense report reads operational pre-GL)
- Explanation: Reversal/disclosure semantics are inconsistent: financial reports net reversals, daily-close subtotals previously counted reversed journals (partially fixed), expense reports read pre-convergence operational data.
- Possible Cause: Independent reporting surfaces built at different times without a shared reversal-disclosure contract.
- Recommended Resolution: Standardize reversal/pre-GL disclosure across all reporting surfaces (see ACC-015).
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-006: CONFLICT-006: Canonical Account vs legacy FinancialAccount "two models" confusion
- Conflicting Documents: accounts/FLOW-ACC-01-CHART-OF-ACCOUNTS.md:28-34 (canonical Account/LedgerAccounts authoritative) vs FinancialAccountsController/FinancialAccount (legacy shadow with AccountNumber + soft-delete)
- Explanation: Two different models/tables coexist; `FinancialAccount` is a legacy shadow with its own CRUD, not the ledger source of truth. Readers may conflate them.
- Possible Cause: Legacy financial-account CRUD predating the canonical CoA.
- Recommended Resolution: Deprecate/label the legacy model clearly; migrate needed capability into the canonical model (see ACC-022).
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-007: CONFLICT-007: Stale "commented legacy writes" label in FLOW-ACC-07
- Conflicting Documents: accounts/FLOW-ACC-07-EXPENSES.md (describes commented legacy writes) vs actual code (controller fully migrated; only a read-only `JournalEntries.AnyAsync` exists at :548)
- Explanation: The doc label no longer reflects reality; no legacy write code remains.
- Possible Cause: Doc not updated after the controller migration.
- Recommended Resolution: Remove the stale label (see ACC-051).
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-008: CONFLICT-008: CommerceHub reconciliation currency (EGP) vs tenant currency surface
- Conflicting Documents: 04-payments.md:210-218 (CommerceHub reconciliation authority = EGP only) vs Tenant.Currency / storefront currency settings (different surface)
- Explanation: The two currency surfaces are distinct but easily conflated; a tenant's configured non-EGP currency does not legitimize a non-EGP CommerceHub reconciliation.
- Possible Cause: Different bounded contexts reuse "currency" terminology.
- Recommended Resolution: Document the two surfaces explicitly; enforce per-currency fail-closed payout/settlement (see ACC-065).
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-009: CONFLICT-009: Two RecordRefundAsync APIs (storefront vs CommerceHub)
- Conflicting Documents: PointOfSale.Core/Payments (IPaymentLifecycleService.RecordRefundAsync) vs PointOfSale.Core/CommerceHub (ICommerceHubFinancialLedgerService.RecordRefundAsync)
- Explanation: Two differently-scoped refund methods share a name; conflation risks incorrect refund-accounting wiring.
- Possible Cause: Separate bounded contexts evolved independent refund APIs.
- Recommended Resolution: Clearly separate and document both APIs (see ACC-056).
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-010: CONFLICT-010: Daily close "Z-Report" reversal counting vs financial-report netting
- Conflicting Documents: accounts/FLOW-ACC-10-DAILY-CLOSING.md (breakdown subtotals counted Reversed at face value) vs accounts/FLOW-ACC-08-FINANCIAL-REPORTS.md (reports net reversals)
- Explanation: Within the same reporting family, the Z-Report breakdown overstated reversed sales while the headline expected cash and the financial reports net correctly. Partially remediated (daily close now uses posted only) but the inconsistency pattern persists for disclosure standardization.
- Possible Cause: Separate report builders without a shared reversal contract.
- Recommended Resolution: Standardize reversal disclosure; verify all subtotal breakdowns exclude/offset reversed journals (see ACC-015).
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-011: CONFLICT-011: Party/GL decoupling for POS cash/card is by-design but under-emphasized
- Conflicting Documents: accounts/FLOW-ACC-06-PAYMENTS.md §8/§16 (party balance is a cache; GL is truth) vs the implicit expectation that SOA AR equals GL AR(1410)
- Explanation: Cash/card POS `AccountLedger` entries are informational and not posted to GL AR; only credit sales post to AR(1410). Reconciliation reports that expect GL AR == sub-ledger AR will be wrong for cash/card.
- Possible Cause: Two-tier model documented but not emphasized at the reconciliation boundary.
- Recommended Resolution: Add explicit note in FLOW-ACC-06 that cash/card POS `AccountLedger` is informational (see ACC-016).
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-012: CONFLICT-012: Supplier-payment reversal documented as absent vs order-refund accounting present
- Conflicting Documents: audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md C-02 (order refund accounting IMPLEMENTED; supplier-payment reversal ABSENT) vs the original audit's conflation of "payment reversal" as a single CRITICAL finding
- Explanation: The original audit merged order-refund (implemented) and supplier-payment reversal (missing) into one CRITICAL finding; peer review split them — order refund is implemented (excluded), supplier-payment reversal remains a real MEDIUM gap.
- Possible Cause: Over-broad original finding grouping ERP and order payments.
- Recommended Resolution: Keep the two as separate tracked items; only the supplier-payment reversal (ACC-001) remains open.
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-013: CONFLICT-013: "Collected-order cancellation posts no reversal" — false positive vs true order-payment defect
- Conflicting Documents: audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md C-01 (INCORRECT / excluded as defect) vs AGENT-06 F-06-02 (survives as TRUE defect of the Order-Payment runtime, just out-of-FLOW-ACC-06 scope)
- Explanation: Peer review concluded collected/recognized-payment cancellation is blocked/collapsed and refunds use a separate path, so C-01 is a false positive for the ERP party-payment scope. However, the underlying order-payment-runtime behavior (collected-then-cancelled leaves GL revenue un-reversed) is a real defect of that runtime — it is simply owned by a different canonical flow, not "non-existent."
- Possible Cause: Scope boundary between FLOW-ACC-06 ERP party payments and the order-payment runtime.
- Recommended Resolution: Track the order-payment-runtime cancellation/reversal gap under the order-payments domain (not under Sub-Agent 1's accounting scope), and confirm the admin cancel guard + refund path covers it; do not re-open C-01 as an accounting defect.
- Domain: ACC

### DCONF-014: CONFLICT: Storefront homepage authority — `StoreConfig.pages[home]` vs `HomepageSection`
- Conflicting Documents: platform/storefront-builder-runtime-final-architecture.md + capabilities/content-management/storefront-content-management.md vs systems/storefront/README.md (HomePage.tsx runtime)
- Explanation: POS Admin Storefront Builder publishes homepage sections into `StoreConfig.CustomizationJson.pages[home]` (projected to Mongo `StorefrontConfigDocument.Pages.Home`), but the public homepage SPA renders `/api/homepage/sections` (Super Admin `HomepageSection` content or fallback hero). Tenant admins believe their homepage edits are live while customers see different content.
- Possible Cause: Two homepage-authoring surfaces evolved separately; Super Admin owns global/marketplace homepage sections, POS Admin owns tenant page builder, but the public render binds only to Super Admin sections.
- Recommended Resolution: Pick ONE canonical homepage authority — either make `HomePage.tsx` render `config.pages.home.sections` (retiring the Super Admin homepage engine for tenant homepages) or disable/hide `page=home` in POS Admin builder and fix copy/API comments. Add an architecture test asserting the chosen runtime source, plus user-facing publish feedback naming the actual surface. (Priority: High — confirmed divergence.)
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-015: CONFLICT: Carrier runtime authority — `CarrierConfiguration` vs `CarrierAccount`
- Conflicting Documents: capabilities/commerce/shipping-management.md vs platform/shipping-fulfillment-runtime-final-architecture.md (both describe the same two-table divergence)
- Explanation: Two docs independently confirm the same conflict: checkout/quotes/CommerceHub/shipment-registration/webhook trust read legacy `CarrierConfiguration`, while storefront warehouse-scoped visibility reads `CarrierAccount`. The docs agree on the divergence but neither is "wrong" — the conflict is in the CODE (two authorities), and the docs disagree on the remedy (converge vs demote).
- Possible Cause: A newer lifecycle/governance aggregate (`CarrierAccount`) was added without fully rewiring runtime readers off the legacy `CarrierConfiguration`.
- Recommended Resolution: Choose one runtime authority and converge all readers (see ORD-011). Until then, document both as separate authorities and block UI from presenting one as the other's source of truth.
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-016: CONFLICT: `Order` TenantId existence — code vs `AdminOrderService` header comment
- Conflicting Documents: capabilities/pos-admin/order-management.md (doc validation) vs the `AdminOrderService` source comment
- Explanation: The `AdminOrderService` class header states Order has NO TenantId column and isolation is via ownership joins only. The code shows `Order` implements `ITenantOwned` with a non-null `TenantId` column enforced at DB level. (Implementation-backed doc vs stale source comment — the comment is the false party.)
- Possible Cause: Code evolved to add a TenantId column; the header comment was never updated.
- Recommended Resolution: Correct the `AdminOrderService` comment; keep ownership joins as defense-in-depth but document the real TenantId column. (See ORD-023.)
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-017: CONFLICT: Checkout service name — order-authority checkout (`CheckoutService`) vs lead-capture `CheckoutSession`
- Conflicting Documents: capabilities/pos-admin/checkout-management.md vs 05-orders.md (prior to the capability file)
- Explanation: Before the checkout capability file, the split between the order-authority checkout (`CheckoutService`, which creates the order + payment intent) and the lead-capture `CheckoutSession` (NOT an order) was not made explicit, causing frequent wrong assumptions. The capability file corrects this; older docs understate the distinction.
- Possible Cause: Two distinct concepts (order-producing checkout vs abandoned-cart lead capture) were conflated in prose.
- Recommended Resolution: Keep the dedicated checkout capability file as the source of truth; cross-reference it from `05-orders.md`.
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-018: CONFLICT: Settlement membership — `PaymentSettlementBatch.OrderCount` vs live `PaymentSettlementOrders` lineage
- Conflicting Documents: 05-orders.md (PASS 4 correction) vs older order/finance prose + systems/commercehub/README.md
- Explanation: Older design trusted the denormalized `PaymentSettlementBatch.OrderCount` as settlement-membership proof. The authoritative PASS 4 correction overrides it: current live `PaymentSettlementOrders` lineage is the only membership authority. `05-orders.md` resolves toward the correction, but the README's "settlement count treated as membership truth" scenario flags the residual risk if any path still trusts the count.
- Possible Cause: A metadata field was historically treated as authoritative before lineage-based membership was enforced.
- Recommended Resolution: Enforce live-lineage membership everywhere; fail closed on count-only proof. (See ORD-042.)
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-019: CONFLICT: Storefront marketplace read model source — `CommerceHubMarketplaceOrder` vs storefront `Order`
- Conflicting Documents: _scratch/order-cqrs-crossdomain.md (§7) vs CommerceHub order projection docs
- Explanation: The customer-facing `marketplace_order_read_models` Mongo doc is built from the STOREFRONT `Order` (keyed by storefront `Order.Id`), NOT from the native `CommerceHubMarketplaceOrder` entity (which is projected into a separate `commercehub_orders` collection). If a storefront Order is soft-deleted but a marketplace order still exists, the marketplace read model can linger (no auto-delete), diverging from product/homepage paths that do delete.
- Possible Cause: Two read models for the same commerce concept were built from different source entities.
- Recommended Resolution: Define the canonical source for the marketplace read model; add cleanup/reconciliation when the storefront Order is tombstoned.
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-020: CONFLICT: CommerceHub-native affiliate commission authority — deferred vs implied
- Conflicting Documents: platform/commercehub-final-architecture.md (Missing Capabilities) vs affiliate platform docs
- Explanation: CommerceHub has no native affiliate commission authority in code (deferred to the separate Affiliate certification/runtime). Docs that imply CommerceHub owns commission authority are incorrect; commission is owned by the Affiliate runtime reading snapshots.
- Possible Cause: Marketplace and affiliate monetization are related but owned by separate runtimes; prose can blur the boundary.
- Recommended Resolution: Keep CommerceHub docs explicit that commission authority is deferred to the Affiliate runtime; cross-reference the affiliate canonical doc.
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-021: CONFLICT: Customer identity domains — POS `Customer` vs `OnlineCustomer` vs `CommerceHubCustomer` vs `Affiliate`
- Conflicting Documents: master-data/MD-03-CUSTOMER-MASTER.md + capabilities/pos-admin/pos-customer-management.md vs storefront/CommerceHub customer docs
- Explanation: Four separate customer identities exist with NO mapping/merge code. POS `Customer` ≠ `OnlineCustomer` (storefront) ≠ `CommerceHubCustomer` (marketplace) ≠ `Affiliate`. Docs that conflate them (or that imply a unified customer) are wrong. This also intersects ORD-032 (OnlineCustomerId stamped on orders).
- Possible Cause: Each bounded context evolved its own identity; no cross-domain customer resolution was built.
- Recommended Resolution: Document the four identities as separate; add a cross-reference map; decide whether a unified customer view is a required product feature.
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-022: CONFLICT: "Receivable / receive payment" workflow exists in UI but not in backend
- Conflicting Documents: platform/FLOW-02-deferred-payment-credit-sale-canonical.md (implementation finding) vs SOA "receive payment" UI (`Payments.jsx`)
- Explanation: The SOA UI exposes a "receive payment" button → `POST /Payments/receive`, but the controller is NOT implemented (404). The receivable lifecycle is read-only; settlement-to-zero is not wired. Docs/UI imply a closed loop that does not exist.
- Possible Cause: UI was built ahead of the backend settlement workflow, or the workflow was deprioritized.
- Recommended Resolution: Implement the receive-payment workflow or remove the UI button and document the gap. (See ORD-052.)
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-023: CONFLICT: TikTok / pixel providers enumerated but absent (phantom providers)
- Conflicting Documents: platform/tracking-attribution-runtime-final-architecture.md (enum/config present, dispatcher absent) vs provider UI/config
- Explanation: `PixelProvider.TikTok` and other pixel providers (Snapchat, LinkedIn, Pinterest, GTM, Custom) are present in enums/config but have NO dispatcher/implementation. Only GA4 + Meta CAPI are real. The enum implies support that does not exist.
- Possible Cause: Provider enum was expanded ahead of dispatcher implementation; TikTok is a confirmed phantom.
- Recommended Resolution: Remove or implement the TikTok dispatcher; prune phantom provider enums or clearly mark them unimplemented.
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-024: CONFLICT: Service Marketplace "provider/consumer" framed as cross-tenant, but is intra-tenant
- Conflicting Documents: platform/service-marketplace-participation-runtime-final-architecture.md (Documentation Validation) vs prompt assumptions / possibly older docs
- Explanation: The prompt assumed an "independent POS Admin tenant provides services to another independent POS Admin tenant." Implementation shows provider and consumer are users INSIDE THE SAME tenant; cross-tenant reach is discovery visibility only. Several assumed entities/states (separate Provider entity, buyer entity, provider lifecycle enum, managed Service Categories) do not exist.
- Possible Cause: Marketing/external framing of "marketplace" vs the actual intra-tenant B2B model.
- Recommended Resolution: Correct docs/assumptions to intra-tenant service exchange with globally-discoverable listings; remove non-existent entity/state references.
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-025: CONFLICT: CMS "Homepage divergence" vs "Storefront homepage divergence" — same root, two doc families
- Conflicting Documents: platform/cms-runtime-final-architecture.md (homepage authority divergence, item 6) vs capabilities/content-management/storefront-content-management.md (System A divergence) vs platform/storefront-builder-runtime-final-architecture.md
- Explanation: Three docs describe the SAME homepage-authority divergence (StoreConfig vs HomepageSection) from CMS, content-management, and storefront-builder perspectives. They agree on the divergence but it is a single architectural issue tracked in three places, risking inconsistent remediation ownership.
- Possible Cause: Homepage rendering spans CMS + content-management + storefront-builder; each doc audits its slice.
- Recommended Resolution: Consolidate into one canonical homepage-authority decision (see CONFLICT 1) owned cross-functionally.
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-026: CONFLICT: Best-sellers ranking claimed vs NOT implemented
- Conflicting Documents: platform/search-runtime-final-architecture.md (ranking table: Best-selling = NOT IMPLEMENTED, placeholder CreatedAt desc) vs storefront-builder/product docs implying best-sellers curation
- Explanation: Search ranking has a "Best-selling" row that is NOT implemented — it returns a placeholder `CreatedAt desc` instead of sales-volume ranking. Storefront/new-arrivals/best-sellers/cross-sell/up-sell/recently-viewed curation surfaces are also verified absent as dedicated recommendation engines.
- Possible Cause: Ranking feature was scoped but never built; placeholder masks the gap.
- Recommended Resolution: Implement sales-volume best-sellers or remove the ranking signal; document curation surfaces as absent.
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-027: CONFLICT: Conversational Commerce "Recommendation Runtime" implied but absent
- Conflicting Documents: platform/conversational-commerce-runtime-final-architecture.md (explicitly NOT a Recommendation/Agent/Discovery runtime) vs prompt/marketing vocabulary
- Explanation: The conversational commerce runtime is a single customer-facing chat surface that drafts intent and calls backend authorities; it is NOT a recommendation/agent/product-discovery runtime. Conversation persistence, memory, RAG/embeddings, tool/function-calling, streaming history, and live preview are NOT implemented. The prompt lists fields/capabilities that are absent.
- Possible Cause: "AI commerce" framing implies richer capabilities than the implemented single-model chat path.
- Recommended Resolution: Document the runtime as a thin LLM-orchestration surface only; mark absent capabilities explicitly; decide which (if any) to build.
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-028: CONFLICT: Customer Trust "Runtime" naming vs actual CRUD+guard implementation
- Conflicting Documents: platform/customer-trust-runtime-final-architecture.md (title implies a Trust Runtime) vs its own Documentation Validation (no trust concept, no CQRS, advisory only)
- Explanation: The document is titled "Customer Trust / Moderation / Risk Runtime" but its own findings show there is no trust concept, no reputation score, no warnings/appeals, no events/projections — only synchronous governance + phone-risk guards (CRUD + guard). The name overstates the capability.
- Possible Cause: Aspirational naming; the runtime was scoped down to governance+risk only.
- Recommended Resolution: Rename to "Customer Governance & Phone-Risk Runtime" (or build the missing trust/reputation/warning/appeal capabilities). (See ORD-055.)
- Domain: ORD

### DCONF-029: CONFLICT: C-01 — Tenant `IsActive` vs `Status` authority disagreement
- Conflicting Documents: platform/superadmin-platform-final-architecture.md (TD1, divergence) vs platform/identity-runtime-final-architecture.md (TD2, divergence #3) vs platform/merchant-platform-final-architecture.md (debt) vs `TenantResolutionMiddleware` behavior
- Explanation: All three canonical docs flag `IsActive`/`Status` as a split-brain, yet `TenantResolutionMiddleware` gates on BOTH, and `TenantsController.SetTenantStatus` writes only `IsActive`. The docs describe the problem but the implementation still enforces an inconsistent gate (a tenant with `IsActive=true, Status=PendingSetup` passes).
- Possible Cause: Partial migration from bool to enum never completed; governance and resolution code evolved separately.
- Recommended Resolution: Make `Status` the sole authority (ARC-003/ARC-098); update middleware and all writers; add a migration/backfill.
- Domain: ARC

### DCONF-030: CONFLICT: C-02 — "Subscription/License/FeatureFlag/GlobalCategory/GlobalBrand" entities assumed but absent
- Conflicting Documents: platform/superadmin-platform-final-architecture.md (TD3, "verified 0 matches") vs any brief/guide implying these governance tables exist
- Explanation: The SuperAdmin doc states the brief's assumed governance tables do not exist (verified 0 matches in `PointOfSale.Core`). Any documentation treating them as entities is wrong.
- Possible Cause: Documentation drift from an earlier design brief.
- Recommended Resolution: Remove references to these as entities; track ARC-004 (model explicitly or confirm intentional absence).

### DCONF-031: CONFLICT: C-03 — POS Admin legacy cash entities: "write-blocked" yet "still written by POS"
- Conflicting Documents: platform/pos-admin-domain-architecture-final.md:503-510 (internal statement)
- Explanation: Same paragraph says `TreasuryTransaction`/`VisaTransaction`/`CashboxTransaction` are "write-blocked (real cash truth = ledger)" but then "CashboxTransaction is still written by POS for operational history." Contradictory on whether it is written.
- Possible Cause: Partial refactor; doc captured both old and new behavior.

### DCONF-032: CONFLICT: C-04 — Currency "fail-closed on missing currency" vs hardcoded EGP/USD DTO defaults
- Conflicting Documents: platform/currency-runtime-final-architecture.md §5 Principle 4 ("Silent currency fallback forbidden", `[Obsolete(error:true)]`) vs TD1/TD6 (hardcoded `EGP`/`USD` defaults in DTOs and `CartEvents`)
- Explanation: The runtime's stated invariant is fail-closed (missing currency blocks), but multiple DTOs and `CartEvents.Currency` default to `"EGP"`/`"USD"`, which can mask missing-currency bugs — directly contradicting the principle.
- Possible Cause: Defaults added defensively; never reconciled with the fail-closed principle.
- Recommended Resolution: Remove hardcoded defaults; require explicit currency (ARC-062/ARC-066).
- Domain: ARC

### DCONF-033: CONFLICT: C-05 — "Single General Ledger" narrative vs four parallel ledgers
- Conflicting Documents: MENTAL_MODEL.md / architecture/financial-architecture.md (§1, "PostgreSQL = single system of record", "immutable double-entry Ledger") vs reporting-runtime TD12 / financial-architecture §9 (four separate ledgers: GL, party sub-ledger, Customer/Supplier transactions, CommerceHub financial)
- Explanation: The mental model asserts one SQL authority, but the financial architecture documents four independently-written ledgers with manual reconciliation (`RebuildPartyBalanceAsync`). "SQL is authority" is true per-table, but there is no single reconciled financial truth.
- Possible Cause: Ledgers evolved per-domain; reconciliation is manual.
- Recommended Resolution: Establish enforced reconciliation (ARC-019); document the four-ledger reality explicitly.
- Domain: ARC

### DCONF-034: CONFLICT: C-06 — Affiliate identity isolation: "global/cross-tenant" vs tenant-scoped mental model
- Conflicting Documents: platform/identity-runtime-final-architecture.md (TD9: CommerceHubCustomer & AffiliateAccount are GLOBAL, no TenantId) vs MENTAL_MODEL.md / Storefront guide (tenant isolation mandatory)
- Explanation: The mental model states tenant isolation is mandatory, but two identity realms (CommerceHub customer, affiliate) are explicitly cross-tenant by design. Consumers may wrongly assume tenant scoping.
- Possible Cause: Marketplace requires global customer/affiliate identity; not reflected as an explicit exception in the isolation rule.
- Recommended Resolution: Add explicit exception to the "tenant isolation mandatory" rule for these global identities (ARC-058).
- Domain: ARC

### DCONF-035: CONFLICT: C-07 — "No stored permissions/RBAC" vs `Accountant` role referenced in auth + pos-admin README
- Conflicting Documents: platform/identity-runtime-final-architecture.md (Permission Runtime: "VERIFIED ABSENT", ghost `Accountant` TD3) vs systems/pos-admin/README.md (references `Owner,Admin,Accountant` roles) vs AdminTenantAuthority `TenantActorRoles` (includes `"Accountant"`)
- Explanation: Permissions are verified absent, yet a ghost `Accountant` role is accepted by authority logic and referenced in the pos-admin guide. The guide implies a role that cannot be assigned via the enum.
- Possible Cause: Role added to authority set but never added to `UserRole` enum; README followed the authority set.
- Recommended Resolution: Reconcile role inventory (ARC-052); either add `Accountant` to enum or remove from authority + docs.

### DCONF-036: CONFLICT: C-08 — Affiliate commission "earnings": Mongo summary vs SQL ledger two definitions

- Explanation: The affiliate system guide asserts SQL ledger is authority, but the reporting doc shows the Mongo `summary` uses different definitions and there is no reconciliation endpoint — two money authorities that disagree during Mongo lag.

### DCONF-040: CONFLICT: C-12 — `Cashbox.CurrentBalance` as cash authority vs obsolete shadow field
- Conflicting Documents: architecture/financial-architecture.md (hidden behavior #2: `[Obsolete]` shadow fields; use ledger) vs any code/doc reading `Cashbox.CurrentBalance`/`ExpectedBalance`

- Domain: ARC

### DCONF-041: CONFLICT: C-13 — Affiliate balance "tenant-scoped" vs global `StoreTenantId`
- Conflicting Documents: architecture/financial-architecture.md (hidden behavior #3: commissions global, `StoreTenantId`, not `TenantId`) vs any doc implying affiliate balance is tenant-scoped
- Explanation: Affiliate commission ledger is keyed by `StoreTenantId` (the store that owes), not the affiliate's home tenant. Docs implying tenant-scoping are wrong.
- Possible Cause: Multi-tenant affiliate model; doc simplification.
- Recommended Resolution: Correct docs; document `StoreTenantId` semantics (ARC-072).
- Domain: ARC

### DCONF-042: CONFLICT: C-14 — `CommissionLedger.Paid` reduces balance vs only `Withdrawal` does
- Conflicting Documents: architecture/financial-architecture.md (hidden behavior #4) vs naive reading of payout lifecycle
- Explanation: `CommissionLedger.Paid` is a lifecycle flag and does NOT reduce available balance; only `AffiliateBalanceLedger.Withdrawal` does. Easy to mis-model payout math.
- Possible Cause: Two-ledger design (commission vs balance) not surfaced in higher-level docs.
- Recommended Resolution: Document the distinction; add drift detection (ARC-073).
- Domain: ARC

### DCONF-043: CONFLICT: C-15 — CommerceHub finance "platform overview" SuperAdmin-only vs admin controller need
- Conflicting Documents: platform/reporting-runtime-final-architecture.md (platform overview unreachable from admin controller, SuperAdmin-only) vs systems/commercehub/README.md (admin finance surfaces exist)
- Explanation: The CommerceHub admin finance controller exposes only vendor `dashboard`/`statement`/CSV; `GetPlatformOverviewAsync` is SuperAdmin-only, so admin operators cannot see platform overview despite having finance surfaces.
- Possible Cause: Intentional SuperAdmin boundary; not stated in admin guide.
- Recommended Resolution: Document the boundary or expose a scoped admin overview (ARC-029).
- Domain: ARC

### DCONF-044: CONFLICT: C-16 — "Outbox critical events never dropped" vs hand-maintained critical-type list
- Conflicting Documents: MENTAL_MODEL.md / architecture (outbox critical messages stay live for retry) vs architecture/financial-architecture.md (hidden behavior #8: `_criticalFinancialTruthEventTypes` hand-maintained; unregistered types risk silent loss)
- Explanation: The guarantee that critical financial events are never dropped depends on a manually-maintained list. A new money event not added to the list is treated as non-critical and can be terminal-dropped on failure.
- Possible Cause: List not derived from event markers.
- Recommended Resolution: Derive criticality from interfaces; add startup assertion (ARC-074).
- Domain: ARC

### DCONF-045: CONFLICT: Tenant `IsActive` vs `Tenant.Status` dual source of truth
- Conflicting Documents: platform/identity-runtime-final-architecture.md (Technical Debt #2, §624) vs itself (Runtime Authority / Tenant Identity Runtime §306-315)
- Explanation: The tenant state is represented by both a legacy `IsActive` bool and a `Status` enum. `TenantsController.SetTenantStatus` writes only the bool, while `PrivilegedRefreshTokenRevocationInterceptor` keys user revocation off `IsActive` and the SuperAdmin governance path keys off `Status`. The two code paths can disagree about whether a tenant is active/suspended.
- Possible Cause: Incremental evolution — the bool predates the enum; the interceptor and governance were written against different signals and never reconciled.
- Recommended Resolution: Make `Status` the single authority (see SEC-002); deprecate `IsActive` writes; update the interceptor and `SetTenantStatus` together.
- Domain: SEC

### DCONF-046: CONFLICT: Ghost "Accountant" role in live authorization metadata
- Conflicting Documents: platform/identity-access-management.md §9.2 / §18#2 / §20 vs `UserRole` enum (referenced in same doc §1.2, §9)
- Explanation: Endpoint `[Authorize(Roles="Owner,Admin,Accountant")]` and `AdminTenantAuthority.TenantActorRoles` include `"Accountant"`, but the `UserRole` enum has no `Accountant` value. A role participates in a live authorization boundary yet can never be assigned — the metadata contradicts the role model.
- Possible Cause: Incomplete role extension (an intended `Accountant` role was partially wired into controllers/authority but never added to the enum or assignment flows).
- Recommended Resolution: Add `Accountant` to `UserRole` with assignment + tests, or strip the literal from controller metadata and `TenantActorRoles` (see SEC-001).
- Domain: SEC

### DCONF-047: CONFLICT: Storefront vs CommerceHub `EmailVerified` enforcement
- Conflicting Documents: platform/identity-runtime-final-architecture.md Divergence #8 / Technical Debt #12 vs CommerceHub customer enforcement same doc §487-489
- Explanation: CommerceHub customer login enforces `IsEmailVerified` (403 `EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED` at token validation); Storefront `OnlineCustomer.EmailVerified` is informational/checkout-scoped and NOT gated at login. The same-named field carries different enforcement contracts across realms.
- Possible Cause: Realm-specific verification policies evolved independently; storefront historically permitted unverified-email login.
- Recommended Resolution: Decide and document the storefront contract; either enforce at login or codify the non-enforcement with rationale (see SEC-008).
- Domain: SEC

### DCONF-048: CONFLICT: Dead hardened JWT config vs live legacy cookie path
- Conflicting Documents: platform/identity-runtime-final-architecture.md Technical Debt #1 / Divergence #6 vs §204/§139 (legacy `auth_token` honored)
- Explanation: `JwtMiddlewareConfiguration.AddHardenedJwtAuthentication` is dead code (never invoked), yet the legacy `auth_token` cookie it references is still read/honored in `Program.cs`. The documented "hardened" configuration path does not run, while the legacy compatibility path does.
- Possible Cause: Auth wiring was inlined into `Program.cs`, orphaning the hardened-config class; the legacy cookie read was retained for migration compatibility.
- Recommended Resolution: Delete the dead class or wire it as the single source; remove the legacy cookie read after migration (see SEC-012, SEC-013).
- Domain: SEC

### DCONF-049: CONFLICT: Capability hints vs server-enforced permissions
- Conflicting Documents: platform/identity-access-management.md §3.4 / §18#6 vs §8 (Permission Engine VERIFIED ABSENT)
- Explanation: `NavigationCapabilities` strings are sent to the client as UI hints and can represent permissions the server does not actually enforce via `RequireClaim`. Frontend-visible capabilities can drift from backend-enforced authorization, creating a presentation-vs-truth contradiction.
- Possible Cause: Capabilities were introduced as UX hints before a server permission authority existed; no enforcement bridge was built.
- Recommended Resolution: Either document capabilities as non-authoritative hints with coverage tests, or promote them into a server-side permission authority (see SEC-010).
- Domain: SEC

### DCONF-050: CONFLICT: Settlement header counts (older prose) vs live settlement-lineage rule
- Conflicting Documents: 08-qa-philosophy.md §693-695 (conflict note) vs older settlement prose in payments/orders guides (denormalized `OrderCount`/`PaymentSettlementBatch.OrderCount`)
- Explanation: Older settlement documentation treats denormalized settlement header counts as membership/authority. The live rule overrides this: settlement membership is live SQL `PaymentSettlementOrders` lineage. The 08 doc states this is "resolved" toward the live rule, but the older prose persists and can mislead.
- Possible Cause: Rule refinement after earlier documentation was written; old prose not purged.
- Recommended Resolution: Annotate/purge the older header-count prose so only the live lineage rule remains (see SEC-030).
- Domain: SEC

### DCONF-051: CONFLICT: "Vendor" overloaded — Tenant (governance) vs User (operator)
- Conflicting Documents: platform/identity-access-management.md §1.3/#4 / §18#3 vs POS Admin operator model (§1.2, §3)
- Explanation: In governance, `UserGovernanceRole.Vendor` resolves to the `Tenants` table (a tenant IS the governed vendor), while POS operators are `User`s. The word "vendor" therefore denotes both an isolation boundary (tenant) and a governed participant, conflicting in scope discussions.
- Possible Cause: Marketplace model maps tenant→vendor for CommerceHub participation; the operator vs tenant distinction was not consistently named.
- Recommended Resolution: Disambiguate terminology in docs/glossary and code comments (see SEC-032).
- Domain: SEC

### DCONF-052: CONFLICT: CommerceHub price-override "disabled" vs existence of a controlled-activation path
- Conflicting Documents: 06-security-identity.md §501-540 / §714-748 (#4) vs commercehub-price-override-* certification doc family (controlled-activation / business-model-correction)
- Explanation: The security guide states price override is disabled by multiple independent safety locks and any activation without full PASS 13.6 control list is a defect, yet a family of "controlled-activation" / "business-model-correction" certification docs implies an activation path exists. The tension between "frozen/disabled" and "activation path designed" can be read as contradictory if a partial activation ships.
- Possible Cause: The feature is architecturally designed + frozen but intentionally not activated; the activation docs describe the future gated path, not current state.
- Recommended Resolution: Keep override disabled until PASS 13.6 is fully implemented + certified; add a deploy-time fail-closed guard; clarify in docs that activation docs are forward-looking, not current behavior (see SEC-021).
- Domain: SEC

### DCONF-053: CONFLICT: Browser-Knowledge YAMLs violate the ontology's "browser-observable only / declare-only-delta" mandate
- Conflicting Documents: `HERMES_BROWSER_ONTOLOGY.md` §6–7 vs `browser-knowledge/FLOW-ACC-*.yaml`, `browser-knowledge/FLOW-01..05*.yaml`
- Explanation: The ontology whitepaper and `ARCHETYPE_RELATIONSHIPS.md` §3 are explicit: Browser Knowledge is "the thinnest layer," declares ONLY page-specific delta, MUST NOT assert backend internals, and reasons ONLY over browser-observable state (DOM, network, storage, console, navigation, visual). Every one of the 19 YAMLs instead embeds deep backend implementation detail: SQL table names (`AccountLedger`, `LedgerJournalEntries`, `LedgerLines`), EF entities (`PartyLedgerAdjustment`, `CustomerTransaction`), service classes (`FinancialPostingService`, `LedgerService`, `CommerceAccountingService`), transaction semantics (`FOR UPDATE / UPDLOCK,ROWLOCK,HOLDLOCK`), outbox mechanics, and `runtime_models`/`runtimes` sequences of internal services. This is the opposite of "declare only the delta."
- Possible Cause: The `shopixy-browser-knowledge` skill that generated the YAMLs ingested implementation-backed accounting discovery docs (`accounts/FLOW-ACC-*.md`) and copied their internals rather than producing true archetype-deltas.
- Recommended Resolution: Either (a) rewrite the YAMLs to honor the ontology (route, archetype reference, page-specific fields, NON-EXISTENCE assertions, browser assertions only, no `runtime_models`/`runtimes`/`expected_runtime_after_success` backend sections), or (b) formally amend the ontology to permit a separate "implementation-backed" YAML tier and rename/scope these files so they are not mistaken for Hermes browser-test deltas.
- Domain: HER

### DCONF-054: CONFLICT: Browser-Knowledge YAMLs never declare an `archetype` and never supply a true delta
- Conflicting Documents: `PAGE_ARCHETYPE_DISCOVERY.md` §8 ("Create a thin Browser-Knowledge YAML that declares `archetype: <name>` and supplies only page-specific deltas") vs all 19 `browser-knowledge/*.yaml` (grep confirms ZERO `archetype:` declarations)
- Explanation: The discovery doc defines the intended YAML contract precisely (Section 8.1–8.4, with worked examples). None of the produced YAMLs declare an archetype, none reference a canonical archetype document, and none are "thin" — they are 312–400 line monoliths. Hermes, per the ontology, is supposed to inherit behavior from the referenced archetype; with no archetype declared, the inheritance contract is unfulfilled and the files cannot be consumed as designed.
- Possible Cause: The generation skill produced standalone knowledge files, not ontology-compliant deltas.
- Recommended Resolution: Add an `archetype:` key + page-specific delta to every YAML, or remove them from the "Browser Knowledge" layer and reclassify as implementation reference docs.
- Domain: HER

### DCONF-055: CONFLICT: YAML `Source of truth` headers cite non-existent canonical files for FLOW-01..05
- Conflicting Documents: `browser-knowledge/FLOW-01-SALES-KNOWLEDGE.yaml` (et al.) header `# Source of truth: .ai/hermes/accounts/FLOW-01-SALES.md` vs actual repository layout
- Explanation: The five sales/purchase YAMLs cite `accounts/FLOW-01-SALES.md`, `accounts/FLOW-02-CREDIT-SALES.md`, `accounts/FLOW-03-SALES-RETURN.md`, `accounts/FLOW-04-PURCHASE.md`, `accounts/FLOW-05-PURCHASE-RETURN.md`. None of these files exist in `accounts/`. The real canonical docs live elsewhere: `business-flows/FLOW-01-CASH-SALE.md`, `platform/FLOW-02-deferred-payment-credit-sale-canonical.md`, `business-flows/FLOW-03-SALES-RETURN.md`, `business-flows/FLOW-04-PURCHASE-INVOICE.md`, `business-flows/FLOW-05-PURCHASE-RETURN.md`. Every one of the five source-of-truth citations is broken.
- Possible Cause: Hard-coded path assumption in the generation skill (assumed an `accounts/` home for all FLOW docs).
- Recommended Resolution: Correct the header paths to point at the real canonical files; add a CI/lint check that every `Source of truth:` link resolves to an existing file.
- Domain: HER

### DCONF-056: CONFLICT: Accounting audit "0 false positives" (FALSE-POSITIVE.md) reversed by peer review (ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md)
- Conflicting Documents: `audits/_scratch/AGENT-FALSE-POSITIVE.md` ("No genuine false positives. Every finding is implementation-backed. SURVIVES: 54") vs `audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md` §11 ("The original '0 findings rejected' conclusion is no longer accurate… 2 INCORRECT… 7 downgrades… corrected evidence for H-02, H-05, M-16, M-04, M-19, L-09")
- Explanation: The false-positive elimination subagent concluded all 54 findings survived. The master peer-reviewed audit explicitly rejects two as incorrect (C-01 collected-order cancel with no reversal; order-refund accounting "missing") and corrects the evidence/severity of many others (e.g., Stripe/Paymob DO have webhook logs; cashbox closure audit is awaited-not-fire-and-forget; negative expenses do NOT post wrong-sign GL). The two reports contradict each other on the validity of the same findings.
- Possible Cause: The false-positive agent did not re-verify against the broader implementation (it only re-read cited lines) and the master audit performed deeper code review.
- Recommended Resolution: Reconcile the two reports; mark the 2 rejected findings and the corrected severities as authoritative in the consolidated audit; do not let the "0 false positives / 54 survive" claim propagate.
- Domain: HER

### DCONF-057: CONFLICT: FLOW-ACC-06 canonical scope vs audit task file-list (scope mismatch acknowledged but unresolved)
- Conflicting Documents: `accounts/FLOW-ACC-06-PAYMENTS.md:6` (explicitly scopes OUT order-payment runtime) vs `audits/_scratch/AGENT-06-PAYMENTS.md` (audited the Order-Payment runtime exclusively)
- Explanation: The canonical doc excludes `PaymentLifecycleService`, gateway adapters, and CommerceHub payments, yet the audit task mapped exactly those files to it. The auditor flagged this as F-06-01/H-01 (HIGH) and recommended splitting ERP-party-payments from the Order/CommerceHub payment runtime into separate canonical flows/doc maps. The remediation report lists this as "VERIFIED, DOCUMENTATION DEFERRED" with remaining work "Split FLOW-ACC-06 docs by ERP party payments vs CommerceHub/order payments" — i.e., still unresolved.
- Possible Cause: Knowledge-base doc boundary and audit task generation were not synchronized.
- Recommended Resolution: Execute the documented split; create separate canonical flow docs (ERP party payments vs order/CommerceHub payment runtime) and update TASK_ROUTING accordingly.
- Domain: HER

### DCONF-058: CONFLICT: Cross-cutting survey precedence vs ontology precedence
- Conflicting Documents: `INDEX.md` "Authority Precedence" (`.ai-context/*` › Domain Guide › `docs/architecture/*` › Source code › README) vs `AUDIT-METHODOLOGY.md` ("IMPLEMENTATION is ALWAYS the source of truth… if documentation and implementation disagree, IMPLEMENTATION WINS")
- Explanation: The Knowledge-Base index places source code *below* documentation and `.ai-context/*`, while the audit methodology (which the accounting audits followed) places implementation *above* documentation. For an agent reasoning about browser/QA behavior these two precedence rules point in opposite directions when doc and code disagree.
- Possible Cause: Different documents written for different audiences (navigational index vs audit methodology) without reconciling precedence.
- Recommended Resolution: Document a single, explicit precedence rule distinguishing "durable architecture QA truth" (where `.ai-context`/guides win) from "implementation-discrepancy investigation" (where code wins), or merge into one statement.
- Domain: HER

### DCONF-059: CONFLICT: `ARCHETYPE_RELATIONSHIPS.md` says knowledge layer is "future `*KNOWLEDGE.yaml`" but they now exist and violate the layer's own rules
- Conflicting Documents: `ARCHETYPE_RELATIONSHIPS.md` §1 ("Knowledge layer — future `*KNOWLEDGE.yaml` … declare only page-specific deltas") vs the existing `browser-knowledge/*.yaml` (monolithic, backend-internal)
- Explanation: The relationship spec anticipated a clean future knowledge layer; the layer now physically exists but does not conform to the described shape, so the document's description of the layer is now inaccurate.
- Possible Cause: Layer shipped before its conformance was validated against the spec.
- Recommended Resolution: Update the relationship/ontology docs to describe the *actual* YAML shape, or fix the YAMLs (see CONFLICT 1/2).
- Domain: HER

### DCONF-060: CONFLICT: INDEX.md "Authority Precedence" omits the `.ai/hermes/audits/` and `accounts/` trees and the `browser-knowledge` layer entirely
- Conflicting Documents: `INDEX.md` (Knowledge Map + reading order covers only 02–08 core guides + systems + certifications) vs actual repo (contains `accounts/`, `browser-knowledge/`, `audits/`, `business-flows/`, `platform/`, `systems/`, `_scratch/`)
- Explanation: The index's reading order and knowledge map do not mention the accounting flow docs, the browser-knowledge YAMLs, or the audit tree. Hermes has no canonical routing instruction telling it these layers exist or which wins when they disagree with a core guide.
- Possible Cause: Index predates the accounting/browser-knowledge/audit layers.
- Recommended Resolution: Extend INDEX.md and TASK_ROUTING.md to register `accounts/`, `browser-knowledge/`, `business-flows/`, `platform/`, and `audits/` as first-class layers with explicit precedence among them.
- Domain: HER

### DCONF-061: CONFLICT-001: Two in-KB audits disagree on financial-architecture documentation readiness
- Conflicting Documents: `audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md` (peer-reviewed FINAL VERDICT) vs `audits/financial-completeness-audit.md`
- Explanation: The peer-reviewed accounting audit concludes "No surviving CRITICAL accounting defect ... suitable as an external ERP architecture review input" (with the caveat of governance/reconciliation gaps). The independent completeness audit returns VERDICT: **FAIL** for the canonical `financial-architecture.md`, stating it is incomplete and overbroad and that money can move unexplained. Both live in the same KB and assess the same financial documentation/implementation.
- Possible Cause: Different scopes — the accounting audit peer-review corrected overstated payment findings and judged the *double-entry engine*; the completeness audit judged the *canonical financial doc's coverage* of every authority/path. The discrepancy is a scope/labeling mismatch, not necessarily a factual contradiction.
- Recommended Resolution: Either (a) explicitly scope the accounting audit as "engine correctness" and the completeness audit as "doc coverage," and state both verdicts side-by-side in INDEX, or (b) complete `financial-architecture.md` per the 16 recommendations and re-score. Do not let agents cite one verdict while ignoring the other.
- Domain: DOC

### DCONF-062: CONFLICT-002: "README is STALE" warning vs authoritative system READMEs
- Conflicting Documents: `02-architecture.md` / `03-inventory.md` / `04-payments.md` / `05-orders.md` / `06-security-identity.md` ("the README is STALE ... do not trust it") vs `INDEX.md` / `TASK_ROUTING.md` (granting `systems/*/README.md` authoritative system-guide status)
- Explanation: The core guides' blanket "README is STALE" statement refers to the PointOfSale repository top-level README, but the word "README" is also the exact filename of the authoritative `systems/*/README.md` system guides. A reader following the core guides can distrust the very system guides the INDEX declares authoritative.
- Possible Cause: Terminology collision; the core guides were written before/independent of the `systems/` README convention and never qualified which README they meant.
- Recommended Resolution: Replace every "the README" with "the repository top-level README (distinct from the authoritative `systems/<name>/README.md` system guides)" and add a one-line clarification in INDEX.
- Domain: DOC

### DCONF-063: CONFLICT-003: Party sub-ledger is either a secondary note or a primary authority, depending on the doc
- Conflicting Documents: `02-architecture.md` + `audits/ACCOUNTING-ARCHITECTURE-AUDIT.md` (GL/`FinancialPostingService` as the authoritative model, party sub-ledger as a light secondary note) vs `audits/financial-completeness-audit.md` (party `AccountLedger`/`LedgerService` is a live first-class financial authority that, undocumented, makes the canonical doc FAIL)
- Explanation: The architecture-centric docs frame the GL as the single truth and mention the party sub-ledger only parenthetically. The completeness audit treats the same sub-ledger as a co-equal authority that must be fully documented; its absence is scored as a documentation failure. The two positions disagree on whether the dual-ledger is a primary architecture that requires first-class documentation.
- Possible Cause: `02-architecture.md` predates the detailed financial audits; the completeness audit (later, 2026-07-20) surfaced the sub-ledger as material but the core doc was not updated.
- Recommended Resolution: Upgrade `02-architecture.md` and GLOSSARY to present the two-tier (GL + party sub-ledger) model explicitly (see DOC-018), and have the completeness audit's recommendations folded into the canonical `financial-architecture.md` (see DOC-011).
- Domain: DOC
